# Continuation Prompt — TI-84 Plus CE ROM Transpilation

> ⚠ **Auto-continuation loop active** — Windows Task Scheduler `TI84-AutoContinuation` fires a headless Opus session every **2h** (set 2026-05-11 20:42). Before editing this file in a human session, check `git log --oneline` for recent `auto-session N` commits and consider `schtasks /change /tn "TI84-AutoContinuation" /disable` to prevent conflicts. **Last updated**: 2026-05-17 (auto-session 353 — **4 DELIVERABLES (Codex 1/4 + Sonnet 4, all Opus-verified): (1) ★★★ PORT 0x0009 RULED OUT AS BOOT GATE — probe-phase353-port0009-analysis.mjs tests 4 return values (0xFF, 0x00, 0x02, 0x04). ALL produce identical 270 unique blocks, same furthest PC 0x0158F8, same termination at max_steps. Port 0x0009 is read 14 times in 5K steps (5 reads per LCD init cycle, pattern repeats every ~2203 steps). All reads logged at pc=0x000000 (block-level resolution). The boot code reads port 0x09, writes the value back, then continues regardless. This is a read-modify-write for CPU speed config, NOT a gate. (2) ★★★ PORT 0xD018 RULED OUT AS BOOT GATE — probe-phase353-portD018-analysis.mjs tests 4 return values (0xFF, 0x00, 0x04, 0x80). ALL produce identical 270 unique blocks. Only 12 reads in 5K steps (not 24 — earlier count was over 10K), all from PC 0x005E7A in two bursts of 6 reads at steps 1215 and 3418. Manual ROM disassembly confirms: address 0x0061A0 = LCD panel ID reader (LD BC,0xD018 then 4× IN A,(C), rotates/masks bits, returns combined ID in A). Caller at 0x0061CC checks BIT 4,A to choose LCD init parameters (HL=0x000306 vs HL=0x000E5C). With 0xFF, takes HL=0x000306 path. But the path choice doesn't affect whether boot loops — init completes the same way regardless. (3) ★★ 50K BOOT CONFIRMS STABLE 270-BLOCK PLATEAU — probe-phase353-boot-50k-loop.mjs runs 50,000 steps. Unique block milestones: 142@1K, 270@5K, 270@10K/20K/30K/40K/50K. Last new block discovered at step 2203 (001768:z80). Loop cycle is ~2174 steps/iteration (23 full iterations in 50K steps). Per-cycle I/O: ports 0x1002 (23× OUT 0x06), 0x0003 (23× IN 0xEF), 0x001D-0x0025 (23× LCD timing writes), 0x003A-0x003C (23× timer config), 0x000D (23× IN 0xFF + OUT 0x0F), 0x0005 (23× OUT 0x36/0x76), 0xB020/B024 (23× OUT 0xFF). Step 9000-9050 sample: all at 0x001377 (DJNZ delay dominates step counts within the cycle). (4) ★★ LOOP DIAGNOSIS — probe-phase353-loop-diagnosis.mjs traces 2000 steps. At step 1892+, execution is in DJNZ tight delay at 0x0060B3 (10 FE = DJNZ -2). The LCD init cycle is TOO LARGE for simple cycle detection (>200 distinct block transitions per iteration). The cycle involves ~50+ unique blocks spanning 0x001200-0x006200. Manual ROM analysis confirms: 0x006133 polls port 0xD00D (AND 0xF0, loop until zero — exits immediately since 0xD00D returns 0x00). 0x00613E polls port 0xD00C (BIT 2,A, loop until clear — exits immediately since 0xD00C returns 0x02 with bit 2=0). 0x0060FA writes to port 0xD018 via OUT (C),A with rotated data (LCD SPI commands). BOOT STATE AFTER SESSION 353: 270 unique blocks, ZERO missing blocks, boot loops indefinitely in LCD SPI init. The loop is NOT gated by any single port returning a wrong value — ports 0x0009, 0xD018 ruled out; SPI status ports 0xD00C/0xD00D return values that pass their checks immediately. The loop repeats because the LCD init CODE ITSELF loops back — the backward branch is at the CALL/RET level, not a port-polling gate. The init routine runs a table of LCD commands, and after finishing the table, control returns to a caller that reinvokes it. NEXT INVESTIGATION MUST FOCUS ON: identifying the backward branch (CALL/JP/JR) that causes the ~2174-step cycle to repeat. This is NOT at the port level — it's at the CODE FLOW level. Likely a loop counter, a RAM flag, or a CALL from a higher-level init function that retries. NEXT: (a) ★★★ TRACE CYCLE BOUNDARY — FIND THE BACKWARD BRANCH — The ~2174-step LCD init cycle repeats 23 times in 50K steps. Track block-to-block TRANSITIONS at the boundary where one cycle ends and the next begins. Specifically: what is the LAST block of cycle N, and what is the FIRST block of cycle N+1? Disassemble the transition point to find the backward jump/call. This requires recording the full block sequence over 2 cycles (~4400 steps starting from step 5000) and finding the repeating boundary. (b) ★★★ INVESTIGATE PORT 0x000D (INTERRUPT FLAG REGISTER) — Port 0x000D is read 23 times (once per cycle) returning 0xFF, then written with 0x0F. On real hardware, port 0x0D is likely an interrupt mask or flag register. Reading 0xFF means "all flags set" — the init code reads, then writes 0x0F to clear/mask some flags. If the init loop checks a flag to decide whether to continue, the wrong value from port 0x000D could cause infinite retries. Test: what happens if port 0x000D returns 0x00 instead of 0xFF? (c) ★★★ INVESTIGATE PORT 0xB020/0xB024 AS POTENTIAL LOOP CAUSE — Both ports get 23× OUT 0xFF per 50K steps (once per cycle). Port 0xB020 is an unknown peripheral. If the init code writes to 0xB020 then reads back to verify, the default 0xFF read might always satisfy the check AND cause a retry. Search the ROM for IN instructions reading from 0xB020/0xB024. (d) ★★ DISASSEMBLE THE FULL CYCLE INIT TABLE — The LCD init at 0x006114-0x006145 sends SPI commands from a table. Where is this table in ROM? How many entries? Does the table have a terminator that the code checks, or does it loop based on a counter? Finding the table structure will reveal why the code re-enters. (e) ★★ TRACE CALL STACK THROUGH ONE CYCLE — Record CALL/RET events through one full ~2174-step cycle. What subroutine calls what? The backward branch is likely a RET from the LCD init back to a caller that has a loop (e.g., FOR i=0 to N: CALL lcd_init_table(i)).**)

> Previous: 2026-05-16 (auto-session 349 — **4 DELIVERABLES (Codex 3/3 + Sonnet 1, all Opus-verified): (1) ★★★ BOOT LOOP ROOT CAUSE IDENTIFIED: PLL POLLING — probe-phase349-boot-loop-io.mjs traces ALL IN/OUT over 5000 boot steps. Port 0x28 (PLL) is read 125 times, always returns 0x00 (never locked). The boot code at 0x00069A writes OUT (0x28),0x04 then immediately reads IN A,(0x28) every iteration. The old PLL handler reset remainingReads on every write (even same value), so lock could never be reported. Port 0x03 (GPIO, 0xFF), port 0x06 (flash, 0xD0), and port 0x3D (reset ID, 0x01) also polled repeatedly but are not the blocking condition — only port 0x28 gates forward progress. (2) ★★★ PLL FIX APPLIED + VERIFIED — Modified createPllHandler write() in peripherals.js: only reset remainingReads when value !== lastWrite. Same-value writes (the boot pattern) now allow the lock countdown to complete. Boot now reaches 0x005998 by step 38 (previously stuck at 0x000688 indefinitely). Golden regression PASS (all assertions). However, a SECONDARY loop exists: after PLL locks, execution cycles 0x001AFD→0x000658→...→0x000688 (29 unique blocks, max_steps at 10k). The next blocking condition is likely flash-related (port 0x06 bit 2 check at 0x0006F3). Probe: probe-phase349-pll-fix-verify.mjs. (3) ★★ BOOT LOOP STRUCTURE DECODED — probe-phase349-boot-loop-disasm.mjs disassembles all 16 target blocks. Key findings: 0x001AFA=CALL 0x0058A6 (PLL subroutine), 0x0006F3=IN0 A,(06)/BIT 2,A/JR Z (flash bit 2 check), 0x000704=CP D0 (MBASE check), 0x00080B=CCF/JP NC,0x000066 (NMI re-entry). The loop is formed by the call/return chain between blocks, not a single backward jump. Transition counts show 50 iterations of the full cycle in 2000 steps. (4) ★★ TIMER DOES NOT BREAK BOOT LOOP — probe-phase349-boot-timer.mjs: 100k steps with timerInterrupt:true (200-tick and 1000-tick intervals) produce IDENTICAL results to timer-disabled: 35 unique blocks, no new territory beyond 0x005998, 0 IRQ callbacks observed (timer dispatches to 0x001713 2440 times but doesn't break the polling loop). Timer is irrelevant to boot progression. BOOT STATE AFTER PLL FIX: 29 unique blocks, PLL locks correctly, secondary loop at 0x000688 (flash register polling). The code at 0x0006F3 reads port 0x06, tests BIT 2,A — with flash returning 0xD0 (bit 2=0), JR Z always taken to 0x000704. On real hardware, bit 2 of port 0x06 likely indicates "flash ready" and should become set after PLL stabilizes. NEXT: (a) ★★★ FIX FLASH PORT 0x06 BIT 2 — The secondary boot loop polls IN0 A,(0x06) and checks BIT 2,A. Currently flash handler returns 0xD0 (1101_0000, bit 2=0). After PLL locks, bit 2 should go high (0xD4 = 1101_0100). Modify createFlashHandler to set bit 2 after PLL is locked (read state.pll.locked). This should break the secondary loop. (b) ★★★ TRACE POST-FLASH-READY PATH — Once flash bit 2 is fixed, boot should advance past 0x0006F3. The fall-through (BIT 2 set → JR Z NOT taken) continues at 0x0006FA. Disassemble 0x0006FA-0x000720 to understand what boot does next. May hit new missing blocks or new peripheral gates. (c) ★★★ TEST COORMON BROWSER SHELL — Open browser-shell.html with CoorMon mode enabled, verify the execution loop cycles (even if no LCD output yet). Check that keyboard events produce scan codes in the log. Debug any missing_block terminations. (d) ★★ INVESTIGATE 0x001AFD CONTINUATION — After PLL subroutine returns, 0x001AFD does LD BC,0x0128 then writes to ports 0x1005, 0x01 etc. Trace this continuation path to understand what init steps remain before the OS reaches event loop. (e) ★★ CORRECT MBASE INITIALIZATION — cpu-runtime.js sets MBASE=0xD0 at construction. Real eZ80 resets MBASE to 0x00. This causes z80-mode memory accesses during boot to read wrong addresses. Consider: set MBASE=0x00 by default, let boot code set it to 0xD0 when ready. Impact: may affect golden regression which assumes MBASE=0xD0.**)

Index 0 = 0x058241 (HOME_HANDLER, confirmed). 27 other entries are NEW handler addresses not in the session 341 cxMain direct-write registry. This means the OS has 28 total contexts, far more than the 15 unique direct-write handlers. The table is the AUTHORITATIVE context registry. Key new addresses: 0x03D755 (idx 1), 0x080CA7 (idx 2), 0x0AB667 (idx 3), 0x06C748 (idx 4), 0x08A653 (idx 5), 0x09C883 (idx 6), 0x09C742 (idx 7), 0x09E30C (idx 8), 0x05FE17 (idx 9), 0x0B290B (idx 10), 0x024479 (idx 24). Key flow: PUSH AF/BC/DE/HL → BIT 6,(IY+27) → if clear: clear 0xD005F6 (callback ptr), set 0xD005F5=1 (countdown), CALL 0x03D1C3 (countdown decrement + optional callback invoke via 0x0A32F9), EI, SET 0,(IY+18) → JP 0x0A1A36 (POP HL/DE/BC/AF + RET). The scan code is ALREADY in A before this call — loaded at 0x08C39C: LD A,(0xD0058C). So 0xD0058C is the authoritative GetCSC result location. The OR A at 0x08C3AC tests the SAME A that went in. Probe: probe-phase342-scan-code-provider.mjs. (4) ★★ HANDLER IDENTIFICATION — 16 handlers disassembled (first 10 instructions each). Pattern: most handlers start with CP cascades comparing the incoming A (scan code) against immediate values, then JP to action routines. 0x058241 (HOME) confirmed: starts with LD HL,0 / RES 7,(IY+82) / LD A,3. 0x06B4E8 has the densest CP cascade (0x27, 0x17, 0x19, 0x15, 0x18, 0x20, 0x09). 0x080FB7 writes 0xD0058C with value 3. All handlers have key-dispatch entry patterns — consistent with the CoorMon architecture where cxMain handlers receive the scan code in A and dispatch accordingly. Probe: probe-phase342-handler-identify.mjs. NEXT: (a) ★★★ INTEGRATE COORMON EVENT LOOP INTO BROWSER SHELL — now that the full architecture is decoded (sessions 338-342), design requestAnimationFrame loop. Prerequisites known: IY=0xD00080, scan code at 0xD0058C, cxMain=0x058241, 28 contexts in table at 0x08C958. (b) ★★★ IDENTIFY ALL 28 CONTEXT TABLE HANDLERS — disassemble first 40 bytes of each of the 28 entries from the 0x08C958 table (not just the 15 from direct writers). Cross-reference scan code CP values with TI-84 CE keyboard matrix to identify which app/screen each context represents. (c) ★★★ TRACE KERNEL INIT ALTERNATIVE PATH — the boot probe shows execution goes to 0x001768 → 0x000804 → 0x000066 → 0x0008BB → 0x0019B5 instead of the expected 0x009006 path. Diagnose WHY: what branch condition at 0x0006A9 (or wherever) diverts execution? May need additional seeds or RAM initialization. (d) ★★ MAP DEFERRED CALLBACK SYSTEM — 0x0A27DD writes 0xD005F5/0xD005F6, calls 0x03D1C3 (countdown), then 0x0A32F9 when countdown expires. What callbacks get registered? Who writes to 0xD005F6? This is the OS's deferred-event mechanism. (e) ★★ DECODE 0x08C9AC INITIALIZATION BLOCK — the code prologue after the context table copies 40 bytes from 0x08CA09 to 0xD031CB. What does this initialization data contain? It runs after context table lookup, likely initializes per-context RAM state.**)

> Previous: 2026-05-16 (auto-session 340 — **4 DELIVERABLES (Codex 3/3 + Opus direct investigation): (1) ★★★ CXMAIN DISPATCH MECHANISM FULLY DECODED (Opus direct ROM analysis + Codex P3 probe, verified). The OS dispatches to context handlers via 0x08C745 = JP (HL) trampoline (single-byte instruction, 15 callers). Primary dispatch wrapper: 0x08C72F = PUSH HL / CALL 0x05622E / PUSH HL / LD HL,(0xD007CA) / CALL 0x08C745 / LD IY,0xD00080 / POP / POP / RET. Called from 0x04EA9E, 0x08C439 (notification broadcast loop with DJNZ), 0x08C536 (main key dispatch after CALL 0x022331 token deposit). Secondary tail-call dispatch at 0x0AB6FA: LD HL,(cxMain) / RES 4,(IY+0x19) / JP (HL). Context handler TABLE at 3-byte stride: cxMain(0xD007CA) dispatched by 0x08C739, cxPutAway(0xD007CD) by 0x08C693, cxRedisp(0xD007D0) by 0x08C6A8, cxHandler3(0xD007D3) by 0x08C6FE, cxHandler4(0xD007D6) by 0x08C725, cxHandler5(0xD007D9) by 0x08C716, cxHandlerB(0xD007EB) by 0x08C74A. Full ROM scan: 47 references to 0xD007CA — 16 LOAD (reads), 19 STORE (writes), 12 ADDR loads (passing address for block copy). Most LOAD sites are CP comparisons (LD HL,(cxMain) / LD DE,<handler> / CALL 0x04C973), NOT dispatch. Only 2 true dispatch sites: 0x08C72F (wrapped) and 0x0AB6FA (tail-call). Probe: probe-phase340-cxmain-readers.mjs. (2) ★★★ KERNEL INIT TERMINATES AT MISSING_BLOCK 0x00001A:Z80 — NOT A HALT INSTRUCTION (Codex P1 probe, Opus-verified). Per-instruction trace of 707 blocks reveals: kernel init at 0x0006C1 calls RST 0x38, RST handler at 0x000038 (EX AF,AF' / EXX / PUSH IX / PUSH IY / LD IY,0x0080 / RET NC) returns to address 0x00001A in z80 mode. No transpiled block exists at 0x00001A:z80 → missing_block termination. This is NOT a hardware halt at 0x0019B5 — it's a MISSING SEED. Fix: add z80-mode seed at 0x00001A. PLL path confirmed working (0x00069A → 0x0006A9). Full port I/O trace captured: OUT 0x01=0x00, OUT 0x1005=0x02, OUT 0x07=0x02, OUT 0x09=0x02, IN 0x03=0xFF, OUT 0x06=0xD4, IN 0x28=0x00. Probe: probe-phase340-kernel-halt-trace.mjs. (3) ★★ HOME_HANDLER CONFIRMED: NO INTERNAL LOOP (Codex P2 probe, Opus-verified). Disassembly of 0x058241-0x0583E7 (422 bytes, first RET): HOME_HANDLER is a single-pass function. It clears IY+0x52 bit 7, loads A=3, checks IY+0x34 bit 4 (conditional CALL NZ 0x0239B3), RET NZ guard, then IY+0x29 bit 2 check, AND mask on IY+0x3C, calls 0x0800C2/0x058BA3/0x058222, then IY+0x1C bit 6 (JP NZ 0x058A2C), IY+0x09 bit 7 RET NZ, IY+0x0C bit 7/6 checks, calls 0x09DCAA/0x083623/0x083764/0x058D49, then **CALL 0x08BF22 (CoorMon) at 0x0582B8**. After CoorMon: clears 7 IY flags (0x4A/4, 0x5/3, 0x47/1, 0x49/6, 0x25/5, 0x8/1, 0x15/6, 0x1F/2), calls 0x0800EC, then writes to context handler table slots (0xD007C7, 0xD007C8, 0xD007C4 via SIS prefix). No backward JR/JP — looping is in the FULL COORMON at 0x08C331, not in HOME_HANDLER. Other CoorMon callers: 0x05CD62 (CALL NZ — conditional, preceded by BIT 3,(IY+0x33)), 0x05CD67 (unconditional CALL), 0x040CAD (in boot/wake init 0x040892). Probe: probe-phase340-home-handler-disasm.mjs. (4) ★★ FULL OS EVENT LOOP ARCHITECTURE NOW UNDERSTOOD (synthesis from sessions 338-340). Architecture: Full CoorMon (0x08C331) = master OS loop. On each iteration: (a) setup + status bar update, (b) dispatches to cxMain via 0x08C72F → JP (HL) → HOME_HANDLER, (c) HOME_HANDLER does home-screen-specific setup then calls narrow CoorMon (0x08BF22) for GetCSC + VRAM refresh, (d) narrow CoorMon returns, HOME_HANDLER writes context table slots and returns, (e) full CoorMon does notification broadcast (0x08C439 DJNZ loop) + key dispatch (CP cascade at 0x08C44A → token deposit 0x022331 → cxMain dispatch 0x08C532), (f) JP 0x08C331 (loop). Key dispatch CP cascade at 0x08C44A: scan code → token mapping, special keys (0x7F, 0xFE, 0xFC, 0xFA, 0xFB, 0xDA) handled inline, default → CALL 0x022331 (token deposit) + CALL 0x08C72F (redispatch to handler). Golden regression 26/26 PASS. Workflow probe unchanged (still fails — execution terminates at missing_block). NEXT: (a) ★★★ ADD Z80-MODE SEED AT 0x00001A — this is the critical fix for kernel init. The transpiler needs to generate a block at 0x00001A for z80 mode execution. This will likely unblock natural boot, allowing cxMain to be populated organically. Run transpiler after adding seed, then re-test kernel init. (b) ★★★ INTEGRATE COORMON EVENT LOOP INTO BROWSER SHELL — now that the full architecture is decoded (sessions 338-340), design requestAnimationFrame loop: boot OS, setup cxMain=0x058241/stack/IY, call full CoorMon (0x08C331) repeatedly. (c) ★★★ TRACE FULL COORMON ENTRY 0x08C331 → 0x08C449 — the path from entry to the key dispatch CP cascade is ~280 bytes of undecoded setup. Disassemble to understand exactly what happens before cxMain dispatch. This will reveal any additional prerequisites (RAM state, IY flags) for the event loop. (d) ★★ INVESTIGATE 0x08C8CB CONTEXT SETUP — 0x08C8CF loads HL=0x058241 (HOME_HANDLER), then calls 0x08C900 which does BIT 6,(IY+0x36), CALL NZ 0x023A02, sets IY+0x01 bits, calls JP (HL) trampoline. This is the context SWITCH mechanism — how HOME context is installed. Trace fully to understand how cxMain gets populated. ## ★★★ Workflow Probe Mandate (READ FIRST — added 2026-05-14)

**The "plateau" risk**: ROM coverage is 95.92% and excellent infrastructure decoding has been landing every session (soft-float, `_indcall`, `_seqcase`, display callers, scrapMem, ...). But **none of this work has been validated against a real student workflow**. With the school year starting in September, the readiness gauge has to switch from "% bytes decoded" to "can a student do `1+1 ENTER` and see `2`."

**Forcing function**: `TI-84_Plus_CE/probe-workflow-arithmetic.mjs` (added 2026-05-14). Boots to home screen, injects `1 + 1 ENTER`, asserts VRAM changes. **Today it fails loudly** — see "DIAGNOSTIC" output. That failure is intentional; it tells you which decoded piece needs to be wired into the runtime to make a student-facing flow work.

**Every auto-session must**:

1. **Run** `node TI-84_Plus_CE/probe-workflow-arithmetic.mjs` at the start, capture the assertion lines, and note them in the session commit message.
2. **Once per week (every ~12th session)**: dedicate the session to making the probe progress (find the right resume PC, wire the event loop, deliver the IRQ, route through `_GetCSC` → token dispatch → display refresh). Use the existing decoded infrastructure — that's what it's for.
3. **Add new workflow probes** as earlier ones go green. Suggested ladder:
   - `probe-workflow-arithmetic.mjs` — `1+1 ENTER` shows `2` ← current
   - `probe-workflow-list-entry.mjs` — `STAT`, EDIT, enter `{1,2,3}` into L1
   - `probe-workflow-1var-stats.mjs` — `STAT`, CALC, 1-Var Stats L1 produces mean=2
   - `probe-workflow-y-equals.mjs` — `Y=`, enter `X`, `GRAPH` produces diagonal line
   - `probe-workflow-distr.mjs` — `2ND DISTR`, normalcdf, produces value

**Why this changes the cadence, not the work**: Sessions stay focused on decoding (that's what the auto-loop is good at). The probe just supplies a *forcing function* — when something blocks, the diagnostic tells you exactly which decoded piece to wire next, and progress against student-facing readiness becomes measurable, not aspirational.

**Do NOT delete or weaken this section**. If a probe goes green, mark it ✅ and add the next one. If a probe regresses, that's a higher-priority bug than any decoding target.

---

## ★ Strategic Reframing (READ FIRST)

**The 16.65% "plateau" is a denominator artifact**, not a reachability problem. ROM is 4 MB but **82.6% (3,464,815 bytes) is erased flash (0xFF fill)** — fundamentally uncoverable. The genuine executable+data ROM is **729,489 non-erased bytes**, and the transpiler already covers **95.92% of them**.

Of the remaining ~30 KB of uncovered non-erased bytes:

| Verdict | Bytes | % |
|---------|-------|---|
| CODE? (decodable) | ~13 KB | 29% |
| DATA-MIXED | ~24 KB | 52% |
| DATA-SPARSE | ~6 KB | 13% |
| STRINGS | ~3 KB | 6% |

Only ~13 KB is plausibly executable code, scattered across ~3,400 tiny ranges (top hole = 62 bytes). **Coverage work is effectively done.** Future sessions should pivot to feature work. Use `audit-true-uncovered.mjs` for true coverage, not `coveragePercent`.

CALL 0x068B78 is a type validator that sets OP2=1.0. Exponent bounds check (0x82-0x84). Falls into body. (2) **OUTER LOOP STRUCTURE DECODED** — Steps 333-734 are post-inner-loop processing. JR NZ at step 332 does NOT go to 0x068D5D — execution continues at 0x068DA3. Loop top re-entry only at step 735 for iteration 2. OP5→OP2 at step 570 is in the OUTER region. (3) **OP4 PRE-LOADING DISPROVEN** — OP4=8.0 and OP4=800 both still produce E_Domain. The OP4→OP2 copy is intentional (restores saved OP1 for InvSub), but the bug is that BOTH InvSub operands end up being OP1 because the compound OP1→OP2 call at 0x07C747 destroys the divisor.

### 1. ★★★ Integrate ParseInp into browser-shell as "real parser" backend
**Session 200 update**: **★ ROM PARSER INTEGRATED INTO BROWSER-SHELL**. Modified `browser-shell.html` to add "Use ROM Parser (PushErrorHandler)" toggle (default OFF). Implementation: separate cached runtime (cold boot + kernelInit + memInit + CreateReal), BufInsert token insertion via 0x05E2A0, PushErrorHandler(0x07C88B) with HL=0x099929, ParseInp(0x099914) with z80 RET intercept at block 0x03E1B1→set PC=0x03E1CA+madl=1, BCD float→JS conversion from OP1. Falls back to eval() on failure. 554 lines added, all constants named (ROMP_ prefix). **Needs human browser test** to verify "2+3" → "5" with toggle ON. **Trig still blocked** (recovery loop, sessions 192-193). **NEXT**: (a) **Human browser test**: verify ROM Parser toggle works for basic arithmetic. (b) If arithmetic works, try more complex expressions (nested parens, negative numbers). (c) Trig requires non-error ParseInp path — deprioritized.
**Session 206 update**: **★ KEY ARCHITECTURAL INSIGHT — Digit tokens DON'T use the BufInsert path at 0x058AC9**. Probe `probe-phase206-cxmain-bufinsert-why.mjs` identified the gate: `CALL 0x0802C4` at 0x058AC9 does `CP 0x5A; RET C` — A (token class from 0x058D54) must be ≥ 0x5A (90) for BufInsert. Digit class = 0x00 → carry → immediate RET. 11 initialization experiments (appContext, IY flags, edit pointers) ALL produce identical 54-step/44-block paths — A value is the sole determinant. **Digits must insert via a DIFFERENT path**: likely CP 0x09 (class 9?) at 0x058A0C, CP 0x0C (class 12?) at 0x058A60, or CP 0x0D (class 13?) at 0x058A6E. Or the actual digit insertion happens BEFORE 0x0589E5 in the cascading CP chain at 0x05877A.
**Session 213 update (P2)**: **0x080D08 DISPLAY RESULT LOOP NEEDS MORE STATE**. Probe `probe-phase213-display-result-loop.mjs`: seeded format buffer at 0xD0060E with "42.5\0", edit pointers initialized, BIT 4 set. Result: BufInsert fires 1× but loop doesn't iterate — edit buffer stays zeroed, cursor at 0xD00A00 unchanged. Even empty string test hits BufInsert once (29 steps all cases). **The block at 0x080D08 doesn't iterate with just format buffer + edit pointer seeding** — likely needs HL register explicitly set to 0xD0060E at entry, or additional OS display context. The LD HL,0xD0060E instruction may be at 0x080D09 (after block entry), or the block may need different entry conditions.
**Session 219 update**: **(n) ★ 0xD003E0 BIT 3 GATE INVESTIGATED** — 11 ROM refs (1 WRITE, 2 READ, 8 POINTER). Sole writer 0x04EE3E: `SET 4,A / LD (0xD003E0),A`. Does NOT set BIT 3 directly — A must already have BIT 3 set when this instruction executes. 0x051D52: `RES 2,(HL) / RES 3,(HL)` CLEARS bits 2+3. 3 BIT 3 test sites: 0x04EABD, 0x04EB66 (the gate), 0x051CF3. Boot=0x00, event loop doesn't write. (o) **★ ALTERNATE PATH (BIT 4 CLEAR) CONFIRMED EQUIVALENT TO BUFINSERT** — 0x05E307→0x05E654 chain writes correct tokens and advances cursor identically. Multi-digit/letter/operator all work. Divergence at block index 11 (0x05E642 vs 0x05E6A4). 16 unique blocks in alternate vs 8 in BufInsert. **Both paths are functionally interchangeable for single-byte tokens.**
**Session 220 update**: **(n-continued) ★ 0x04EE33 IS THE REAL ENTRY (NOT 0x04EE1B)**. Probe `probe-phase220-04ee1b-bit3-armer.mjs`: 0x04EE1B is just `JR 0x04EE00` → dead end. Actual D003E0 writer entry is 0x04EE33: `PUSH AF / CALL 0x052004 / POP BC / LD A,B / JR Z,0x04EE3E / SET 4,A / LD (0xD003E0),A`. **BIT 3 NEVER SET in this function** — A passes through UNCHANGED from caller. Only 2 call sites: 0x04E9FD (`CALL 0x04EE33`) and 0x04EDDF (`JP Z,0x04EE06` — doesn't reach 0x04EE3E). Caller at 0x04E9FD: `LD B,A` at 0x04E9E9, `LD A,(0xD0058E)` at 0x04E9EA (saves to C), `LD A,B` at 0x04E9EF, `CALL 0x051B2B`, `RET C` (gate), `LD A,B` at 0x04E9FC, `CALL 0x04EE33`. A clamped to 0xFE if 0xFF. **BIT 3 source is whatever A value the function containing 0x04E9FD receives**. Event loop: 0 writes in 10k steps. ~~**NEXT**: Trace callers of the function containing 0x04E9FD~~ DONE (session 221). (o-continued) **★ 2-BYTE TOKENS TESTED — SECONDARY TABLE NEEDS MODE FLAGS**. Probe `probe-phase220-2byte-token-insert.mjs`: 16 scan codes with primary=0x00 identified (trigger secondary table). 4 tested (0x5B/0x69/0x76/0x7B). ALL write 0x00 to edit buffer — secondary table lookup returns 0x00 without correct mode/context flags (2nd-mode flag, ALPHA state). Double-writer block 0x05E345 NEVER triggered. Both BIT 4 SET and CLEAR paths produce identical results. **CONCLUSION**: 2-byte token insertion requires 2nd-mode or ALPHA-mode context flags to be set before ConvKeyToTok — the table lookup resolves the scan code differently based on mode state. ~~**NEXT**: Test with IY flags armed~~ DONE (session 221): IY flags DON'T control table selection — 0x05C52C (key classifier) does. ~~DONE (session 222)~~: **★★ 0x05C52C uses MAGIC A VALUES (0xFA/0xFB/0xFC/0xFE) for secondary tables, not IY flags. GetKey bridges scan→key code conversion. **Session 204 update**: **★ "STUCK AT 0x092263" WAS A FALSE ALARM — BLOCK EXECUTES FULLY, STAT PIPELINE CONTINUES**. The value 0x1DBA never appears because ADL CALL wrote to 0xD1xxxx, z80 RET reads from 0xD0xxxx — completely different memory. **(MAJOR FINDING) MADL suffix byte is the ONLY correct fix**: On real eZ80, CALL pushes [MADL_byte][PC] and RET pops MADL first to determine caller mode + address width. ADL→z80 mode crossing (via RSMIX) means z80-mode RET must pop MADL byte to know it should switch back to ADL and pop 3-byte address. Without MADL, z80-mode RET pops wrong width from wrong page. Session 173's global attempt broke golden regression by adding byte to ALL CALLs. Targeted approach needed: only emit MADL for mode-crossing CALL/RET. 151 RSMIX sites in ROM, 0 STMIX. ### Completed (session 183)
- ✅ **MAJOR ARCHITECTURAL FIX: eZ80 z80-mode stack MBASE addressing (1 RUNTIME FIX, 1 PROBE, 1 KEY FINDING)**: **(FIX) cpu-runtime.js push/pop z80-mode branch**: Session 172's RSMIX block termination fix (correct for instruction decoding) exposed a deeper bug: z80-mode PUSH/POP/RET were accessing SPL-addressed memory (0xD1xxxx), but the real eZ80 uses {MBASE, SPS[15:0]} addressing in z80 mode. With MBASE=0xD0 and SP=0xD1A87E, z80-mode should access 0xD0A87E (different page). Fix: push() z80 branch now computes `addr = (mbase << 16) | (sp & 0xFFFF)` and only modifies lower 16 bits of SP. pop() z80 branch mirrors this. **Three failed fix attempts first**: (1) global MADL suffix byte for CALL/RET broke golden regression (extra byte on every CALL corrupts all stack frames), (2) MADL suffix + always-3-byte push/pop also broke, (3) always-3-byte push/pop alone also broke (z80-mode matched PUSH/POP pairs need 2-byte width). The MBASE addressing fix is the correct eZ80 architectural solution. **(P1) Error dispatch trace probe**: Codex built `probe-phase183-error-dispatch-trace.mjs`. Traces the full error handler dispatch chain when errNo=0x8D is set. Confirms JError at 0x061DB2 writes errNo, calls 0x03E1B4 wrapper, which calls 0x03E187 helper (RSMIX to z80 mode for port cleanup), z80-mode POP/RET now access {MBASE, SPS} page, execution goes to boot vector → ISR → HALT. **Before fix**: POP/RET at 0x03E1B1 corrupted return address (0xCA00 from stack mismatch) → RST 08h chain → 0x1BC300 erased flash CRASH. **After fix**: z80-mode RET reads from 0xD0xxxx page (zeros → 0x000000 boot vector) → proper ISR path → HALT at 0x0019B5. **(FINDING) ParseInp still gets E_Undefined**: The error handler properly unwinds but doesn't go through JError's LD SP,(errSP) at 0x061DCA, so the PushErrorHandler error frame (hlPayload=0x099929) is never reached. The z80-mode exit path bypasses JError. Next investigation: understand what the real OS puts at the 0xD0xxxx SPS stack page, or whether MBASE should be 0xD1 to match the SPL page. Codex P2 (parseinp-home-context): broken probe, no output. Codex P3 (key-classifier): not created (Codex timeout). Golden regression: 26/26 PASS (unchanged). Coverage: 694,895 (93.26%). Seeds: 50,060. Blocks: 145,846.

## Research-Informed Refinements (added 2026-04-24 from external deep-research pass)

Grounded in TI-83 Plus SDK prose (parser/error-handler contracts), WikiTI 83+ memory-layout page, and the CE include mirror (ti84pceg.inc). Public record has **zero labels** for 0x099211 / 0x099910 / 0x07FF81 / 0x08BF22 / 0x0831A4 / 0x09215E / 0x04C940 — our RE is the source of truth on those. Highlights that inform current priorities:

### R1. ✅ RESOLVED (session 110) — `pTempCnt` audit
pTempCnt at 0xD02596 is correctly zeroed by MEM_INIT (confirmed 0x000000). Stays zero throughout MEM_INIT → CreateReal → ParseInp pipeline and across all expression evaluations. NOT a factor in the RclVarSym failure.

### R2. ✅ RESOLVED (session 111) — Parser flags fully enumerated
Report flags these publicly-named knobs as likely ParseInp-sensitive (all from CE include):
- `ParsFlag`, `ParsFlag2` (including `numOP1` = "result in OP1")
- `onFlags.parseInput`
- `fmtFlags`, `numMode`, `fmtOverride`
- `graphFlags2.splitOverride`
- `newDispF.allowProgTokens`, `progExecuting`
- `hookflags4.parserHookActive` + `parserHookPtr`

**Action**: enumerate each against postInitState. Current probes may accidentally inherit a clean zero-state that works, but any leftover bit from a prior CoorMon iteration would silently change ParseInp's branch. Deterministic seed = deterministic trace.

### R3. ✅ RESOLVED (session 110) — Descending-boundary invariant
Invariant holds after MEM_INIT and after CreateReal, but **VIOLATED after ParseInp** in ALL cases:
- `FPSbase > FPS` (FPS underflows 9 bytes below base — one 9-byte FP value pushed downward)
- `OPS > OPBase` (OPS overflows 6-12 bytes above base — operator stack grows upward past boundary)
This is the root cause of VAT entry corruption (priority 1). ParseInp does not clean up FPS/OPS after evaluation — the OS normally handles this between stages.

### R4. ✅ RESOLVED (session 110) — 0x099211 is NOT ParseInpGraph
Static disassembly of 300 bytes confirms: 0x099211 is a "DisarmScroll" / variable-recall-to-edit-buffer routine. No CALL/JP to ParseInp (0x099914), no trampoline reference (0x099910), no PushErrorHandler (0x061DEF), and ZERO IY ops in the ParsFlag area (IY+0x44 through IY+0x4C). All 8 IY operations target lower offsets (statFlags at IY+0x09, IY+0x01, IY+0x05). Session 98's "DisarmScroll" name was correct. The ParseInpGraph hypothesis is disproved for this address.

### R5. ★ Treat `errSP` as RAM snapshot, not live SP (mental model fix)
TI's 83+ `ResetStacks` comment is explicit: `(onsp)→(errsp), (fpbase)→(fps), (opbase)→(ops)`. `errSP` is a RAM-saved snapshot distinct from the hardware SP. Our session-108 18-byte frame-pop finding is reverse-engineered behavior (TI only publishes "six pushes" + SP/FPS/OPS restore), so brittle by design. Implication: when diagnosing trampoline/SP-shift issues, track `errSP` (RAM) and `SP` (CPU) as **two independent state variables** and log both in error-path probes.

### Completed (session 71)
- ✅ **Phase 25I** — First validated FP engine probes. Built `fp-real.mjs` BCD helper (writeReal / readReal, 9-byte TI format: sign + biased exp + 7 mantissa bytes holding 14 BCD digits). Three probes against cold-boot OS, each asserting BOTH `returnHit` AND numeric tolerance: FPAdd(2+3)=5 exact, SqRoot(4)=2 exact, Sin(π/6)=0.5 exact. **Surprising finding**: cold-boot + CoorMon(0x08C331) + postInitState is sufficient for the entire FP engine — no angle-mode flag, no FPS init, no scratch-register seeding. Opposite of Phase 25H-b (unguarded primitive at JT slot) — here the JT slots dispatch into richer engines that init their own state. **Codex review via cross-agent/v1** caught two real bugs we fixed: (a) initial Sin probe used sin(0)=0, a degenerate false-positive trap — swapped for sin(π/6)=0.5 which actually exercises the transcendental engine; (b) `cpu.push(FAKE_RET)` was relying on `cpu.madl=1` leaking from prior `runFrom('adl')` — now forced explicitly. Both were latent pitfalls for any future probe. Known follow-ups (DONE in session 72): BCD carry-overflow normalization, NaN/Inf handling. Artifacts: `fp-real.mjs`, `probe-phase25i-{fpadd,sqroot,sin}.mjs`, `phase25i-{fpadd,sqroot,sin}-report.md`. Golden regression 26/26 PASS.

### Completed (session 70)

The classic 83+ "refuse if stats not computed" contract is enforced at a higher abstraction layer (parser / token dispatch), not at the JT slot. Probe setup verified trustworthy by Sonnet review (all 5 check points PASS: tMean=0x21, statFlags@0xD00089, statsValid=bit 6, IY=0xD00080, entry disassembly confirms no guard). For raw 0x31: `(3*8)+1+1 = 26 = 0x1A`. Table byte at 0x09F7B5 = 0x90. ISR stage: zero table reads (translation happens in main-loop GetCSC path). Callers: 0x0302EB, 0x02FFAE, 0x02FFBF, 0x02FFDE. ## Key Reference Addresses

### Canonical OS Entry Points (Jump Table, Phase 25H-a)
Full list in `TI-84_Plus_CE/phase25h-a-jump-table.json` (957 named, 23 unnamed). Highlights:

|------|------|------|-----------|
| 0x020148 | 0x03F9CD | `KbdScan` | Keyboard |
| 0x02014C | **0x03FA09** | `GetCSC` | Keyboard (supersedes 0x02FF0B) |
| 0x020150 | 0x08C331 | `CoorMon` | OS main coordinator (our "OS init" ≡ this) |
| 0x0201BC | 0x07C771 | `FPSub` | FP |
| 0x0201C0 | 0x07C77F | `FPAdd` | FP |
| 0x0201D4 | 0x07C8B3 | `FPSquare` | FP |
| 0x0201F8 | 0x07DF66 | `SqRoot` | FP |
| 0x0201FC | 0x0685DF | `RndGuard` | FP |
| 0x02020C | 0x07E053 | `LnX` | FP |
| 0x020220 | 0x07E543 | `SinCosRad` | FP |
| 0x020224 | 0x07E57B | `Sin` | FP |
| 0x020228 | 0x07E5B5 | `Cos` | FP |
| 0x02022C | 0x07E5D8 | `Tan` | FP |
| 0x020294 | 0x07F831 | `CpOP1OP2` | FP |
| 0x0204F0 | 0x08019F | `Rcl_StatVar` | Stats |
| 0x0204EC | 0x09A3BD | `Sto_StatVar` | Stats |
| 0x02050C | 0x08383D | `ChkFindSym` | VAT |
| 0x020534 | 0x08238A | `CreateReal` | VAT |

| 0x0207B8 | 0x0A1B5B | `PutC` | Display (large font) |
| 0x0207BC | 0x0A1C62 | `DispHL_s` | Display |
| 0x020808 | 0x0A21BB | `ClrLCDFull` | Display |
| 0x02080C | 0x0A21C1 | `ClrLCD` | Display |
| 0x020828 | 0x0A235E | `HomeUp` | Display |
| 0x020830 | 0x0A23E5 | `VPutMap` | Display (small font) |

### ROM
- `0x0040ee` — font table base (1bpp 10×14, 28 bytes/glyph, idx = char − 0x20)
- `0x005B96` — VRAM fill primitive
- `0x0802b2` — `SetTextFgColor` (HL→0xD02688, DE=0xFFFF→0xD0268A, sets bit 4 of 0xD000CA)
- `0x0A190F` — glyph byte loader + color flag check (Phase 188)
- `0x0A1A3B` — per-row color pixel subroutine (reads fg/bg from 0xD02688/0xD0268A)
- `0x0A1939`, `0x0A19D7` — VRAM pixel writers
- `0x062160` — generic error banner renderer (48 permutations via 0xD008DF/0xD00824)
- `0x088720` — restore path (copies 260 bytes: 0xD02EC7 → 0xD006C0)
- `0x08C331` — full OS init entry (CoorMon master loop). JP 0x08C331 at end of each iteration. Contains setup, cxMain dispatch, notification broadcast (DJNZ at 0x08C443), key dispatch (CP cascade at 0x08C44A), token deposit (CALL 0x022331 at 0x08C52E), handler redispatch (CALL 0x08C72F at 0x08C532)
- `0x08C745` — JP (HL) trampoline (1 byte). 15 callers dispatch various targets through this single instruction. CALL 0x08C745 with HL loaded = call target, return to caller
- `0x08C72F` — cxMain dispatch wrapper: PUSH HL / CALL 0x05622E / PUSH HL / LD HL,(0xD007CA) / CALL 0x08C745 / LD IY,0xD00080 / POP / POP / RET. Called from 0x04EA9E, 0x08C439 (notification broadcast), 0x08C532 (main key dispatch)
- `0x08C439` — notification broadcast: PUSH BC/HL, LD D=0 E=A, CALL 0x05C5B3, CALL 0x08C72F (cxMain dispatch), RES 4 IY+9, POP HL/BC, DJNZ -26 (loops B times)
- `0x0AB6FA` — cxMain tail-call dispatch: LD HL,(0xD007CA) / RES 4,(IY+0x19) / JP (HL). No return
- `0x08C900` — context switch mechanism. Checks IY+0x36 bit 6, dispatches via JP (HL) trampoline. Called from 0x08C8D8 with HL=handler address
- `0x04C990` — cxCurApp zeroing site (session 92: zeroes cxCurApp at step 18296 when cx manually seeded). Needs disassembly
- `0x058241` — home-screen cxMain handler target (set by 0x08C8CB). 422 bytes to first RET at 0x0583E7. Calls narrow CoorMon at 0x0582B8. 3 conditional RET NZ guards (IY+0x34/4, IY+0x09/7, IY+0x0C/6). Writes context table slots (0xD007C4/C7/C8) via SIS prefix after CoorMon return
- `0x0019BE` — OS event loop entry (ISR: 0x038 → 0x719 → here when 0xD177BA set)
- `0x0159C0` — direct keyboard scan (scan code in B)
- `0x0a2b72` / `0x0a29ec` / `0x0a2854` / `0x0a2106` — home stages 1/3/4/entry-line
- `0x0a349a` — status bar updater (guard: bit6 of 0xD0009b must be clear)
- `0x0a3320` — status indicator dots
- `0x056900` — dispatch table populator (dynamic-only; head=0xD0231A tail=0xD0231D)
- `0x09DEE0` — MEM_INIT entry: heap/VAT allocator initializer. HL=0xD1A881→tempMem/FPSbase/FPS/newDataPtr; HL=0xD3FFFF→pTemp/OPBase/OPS/progPtr. Then calls 0x08A98F, sets IY+71 bit 7, flash-size→0xD025C5. RET at 0x09DF34. Single caller: 0x09DD62.
- `0x0820ED` — compaction/delete helper: BC=9, subtracts from OPBase/pTemp/progPtr, LDIR block move. NOT boot-time init. Entry 0x0820F1 takes arbitrary BC.
- `0x09F79B` — scan code translation table (228 bytes, 4 modifier × 57). Index: `((raw>>4)*8)+(raw&0x0F)+1`. Modifier offsets: none=0, 2nd=0x38, alpha=0x70, alpha+2nd=0xA8
- `0x02FF0B` — GetCSC lookup entry (drives table read). Callers at 0x0302EB, 0x02FFAE, 0x02FFBF, 0x02FFDE
- `0x000028` — rst 0x28 = FP EXIT (di; rsmix → 0x2B → 0x02011C → 0x04AB69 → 0x03AC → 0x19B5 → HALT)
- `0x00B2C4` — FP engine operation selection hub
- `0x00B554` — FP engine loop-back to 0x00AE24

### RAM
- `0xD02688` / `0xD0268A` — fg / bg color registers
- `0xD00595` / `0xD00596` — curRow / curCol (curRow wraps 0xFF via `inc a`, not sentinel)
- `0xD005F8` — OP1, `0xD00603` — OP2, `0xD0060E` — OP3, `0xD00619` — OP4, `0xD00624` — OP5, `0xD0062F` — OP6 (11 bytes apart, NOT 9)
- `0xD005A1-0xD005C5` — per-char font record (IX walks this)
- `0xD006C0` — display buffer (260 bytes, no natural writers — use restore path)
- `0xD0058E` — keyboard OS scan-code output
- `0xD0009B` — system flags (bit6 = status-bar update guard)
- `0xD000c6` — icon type selector (bit2: 0=battery, 1=mode dots)
- `0xD02EC7` — backup buffer (seed for restore path)
- `0xD020A6` — 26-byte mode text buffer ("Normal Float Radian       ")
- `0xD008DF` — errNo (error code register — written by error handler at 0x061DB2)
- `0xD008E0` — errSP (saved SP for error longjmp — MUST seed for CreateReal/ParseInp)
- `0xD008E3` — errOffset
- `0xD007FA` — onSP (ON-key handler SP)
- `0xD00542` — scratch RAM (used by interrupt-safe wrapper at 0x03E1B4)
- `0xD00619` — OP4 (type byte bit 3 tested by CreateReal post-allocation)
- `0xD02587` — tempMem (initialized to userMem=0xD1A881 by MEM_INIT)
- `0xD02590` — OPBase (VAT base pointer)
- `0xD02593` — OPS (parser expression stack pointer — critical input for ParseInp)
- `0xD02596` — pTempCnt
- `0xD0259A` — pTemp (VAT temporary pointer)
- `0xD0259D` — progPtr (VAT program pointer — NOT 0xD01C00 as previously guessed)
- `0xD3FFFF` — symTable (end of VAT symbol table)
- `0xD02577` — InsertMem scratch slot (saved BC for VAT walker)
- `0xD02AD7` — signExtTemp (negated insertion delta, used by pointer adjuster 0x082823)
- `0xD02AD9` — signExtFlag (high byte of signExtTemp, used by 0x04C9A8 for 24-bit right shift)
- `0xD0066F-0xD0067B` — iMathPtr1-5 (3-byte pointer slots, adjusted by InsertMem bulk updater 0x0824FD)
- `0xD0067E/0xD00681` — asm_data_ptr1/2 (also adjusted by InsertMem)
- `0xD0244E` — editSym (pointer to VAT of variable being edited, adjusted by 0x082823)
- `0xD0257B/0xD0257E` — tSymPtr1/tSymPtr2 (temp-symbol pointers, adjusted by 0x082823)
- `0xD02581/0xD02584` — chkDelPtr3/chkDelPtr4 (delete-check pointers, adjusted by 0x082823)
- `0xD0256A` — fmtMatMem (matrix-formatting scratch pointer, adjusted by InsertMem)
- `0xD025A0` — newDataPtr (points into moved VAT data area, adjusted by InsertMem)
- `0xD02317/0xD0231A/0xD0231D` — begPC/curPC/endPC (NOT editCursor/editTail/editBtm — those are at 0xD0243A/0xD0243D/0xD02440)
- `0xD01FEA/0xD01FF0/0xD01FF6` — editor/deletion tracking pointer slots (proposed: editPtr1/2/3) adjusted by 0x0827CA. 6-byte stride, each with +3 scratch companion (0xD01FED/0xD01FF3/0xD01FF9). Seeded from chkDelPtr3, reads via CALL 0x04C973 compare helper. NOT in ti84pceg.inc.
- `0xD02567` — fmtMatSym (pointer to matrix-formatting symbol, adjusted by 0x0827CA)
- `0xD007CA-0xD007EB` — context handler table (3-byte stride, 11 slots): 0xD007CA=cxMain (dispatched by 0x08C739), 0xD007CD=cxPutAway (0x08C693), 0xD007D0=cxRedisp (0x08C6A8), 0xD007D3 (0x08C6FE), 0xD007D6 (0x08C725), 0xD007D9 (0x08C716), 0xD007EB (0x08C74A). cxPutAway/cxRedisp guarded by BIT 5,(IY+0x25) RET NZ. 47 total ROM refs to cxMain: 16 reads, 19 writes, 12 addr loads
- `0xD177BA` — post-init flag (enables 0x719 → 0x19BE ISR path)
- `IX = 0xD1A860` — home-screen stage entry value

### Ports
- `0x3D/0x3E` — IRQ status/ack. `0x5000+` — FTINTC010. `0x500A` — keyboard IRQ ack (OUT 0x08). `0x5006` — keyboard IRQ mask.
- `0xF80000+` — LCD PL111 registers (ROM writes here in ADL mode: timing0 +0x00, timing1 +0x04, timing2 +0x08, upbase +0x10, lpbase +0x14, ctrl +0x18). **Now mirrored in cpu-runtime.js alongside 0xE00000** (Phase 202g). F80000 writes are logged via `cpu.getLcdF80Stats()`.
- `0xE00000+` — LCD MMIO as intercepted by peripherals.js (upbase +0x10, ctrl +0x18). `0xE00800-0xE00920` — keyboard MMIO. `0xE00900` — scan result.
- `0xD40000` — VRAM base (BGR565)

## Operating Mode (Parallel Codex Dispatch)

1. **Pick up state** from this file + `git log --oneline | head -10`.
2. **Dispatch 3–4 parallel Codex agents** per session via `cross-agent.py`. CC orchestrates and verifies; Codex writes files and runs probes.
3. **Keep Codex tasks ≤10 min each** — 15-min timeouts kill broader tasks. Split big investigations into (a) find X / (b) use X.
4. **Sonnet fallback** via Agent tool when Codex stalls with 0-byte output.
5. **CC verifies every deliverable** (re-run `node probe-*.mjs`) before committing.
6. **Commit + push** with message `feat: auto-session N — Phases X/Y/Z (one-line per phase)`.
7. **Update this file** before stopping. Keep only current state + next priorities fresh.
8. **At every pause** run `/context`. <70% of 1M → proceed. ≥70% → stop and hand off.

**Dispatch command**:
```bash
python "C:/Users/rober/Downloads/Projects/Agent/runner/cross-agent.py" \
  --direction cc-to-codex --task-type implement \
  --prompt "<self-contained task: exact addresses, file paths, calling conventions>" \
  --working-dir "C:/Users/rober/Downloads/Projects/school/follow-alongs" \
  --timeout 600
```

Codex has NO conversation context. Prompts must be fully self-contained.

---

## Important Constraints

- **Don't revert dirty worktree files** — many unrelated modifications sit in the tree intentionally.
- **Stay with 1:1-ish bytecode lift** — widen decoder, improve CFG discovery, improve instruction semantics, add runtime helpers only to support lifted blocks. Do NOT rewrite as a high-level emulator.
- **Disable timer IRQ for OS init probes**: `createPeripheralBus({ timerInterrupt: false })`. Default 200-tick IRQ hijacks init via 0x1713 → 0x67F8 → 0x1c33 infinite loop.
- **ROM write-protect essential**: `cpu-runtime.js` `write8/16/24` silently drop writes where `addr < 0x400000`.
- **Keyboard matrix (SDK-authoritative)**: `keyMatrix[N] = SDK Group(7-N)`. Scan code = `(idx << 4) | bit`. 63/64 active; DOWN = 0x00 collides with no-key. Full map in `TI-84_Plus_CE/keyboard-matrix.md`.
- **`mem.fill(val, start, length)` is WRONG** — it's `(val, start, END)`. Always use `start + length`. Fixed across 47 probes in Phase 194.

---

## How To Regenerate

```bash
node scripts/transpile-ti84-rom.mjs                      # ~2s
node TI-84_Plus_CE/test-harness.mjs                      # 25-test harness
node TI-84_Plus_CE/probe-phase99d-home-verify.mjs        # golden regression
node TI-84_Plus_CE/audit-true-uncovered.mjs              # true-coverage audit
node TI-84_Plus_CE/coverage-analyzer.mjs                 # gap analysis
( cd TI-84_Plus_CE && gzip -kf -9 ROM.transpiled.js )    # regenerate .gz
```

After committing code changes, re-run `npx gitnexus analyze` (PostToolUse hook usually handles this). Add `--embeddings` if `.gitnexus/meta.json` shows `stats.embeddings > 0`.

## Key Repo Files

**Transpiler + runtime**: `scripts/transpile-ti84-rom.mjs`, `TI-84_Plus_CE/{ROM.rom, cpu-runtime.js, ez80-decoder.js, peripherals.js}`

**Validation**: `TI-84_Plus_CE/{test-harness.mjs, test-alu.mjs, ti84-math.mjs, coverage-analyzer.mjs, audit-true-uncovered.mjs, probe-phase99d-home-verify.mjs}`

**Browser + automation**: `TI-84_Plus_CE/{browser-shell.html, ti84-keyboard.js, ti84-lcd.js, scancode-translate.js, frontier-runner.mjs}`, `scripts/auto-continuation.bat`

**CEmu trace pipeline**: `cemu-build/{scenario-*.json, run-trace-*.ps1, *-trace.log}`, `TI-84_Plus_CE/cemu-trace-to-seeds.mjs`

**Specs + phase reports**: `TI-84_Plus_CE/phase*-report.md`, `TI-84_Plus_CE/{PHASE25_SPEC.md, PHASE25G_SPEC.md, keyboard-matrix.md, AUTO_FRONTIER_SPEC.md}`

---

## Where History Went

Anything not in this file lives in `git log`. Every auto-session commit is labeled `feat: auto-session N — Phases X/Y/Z` and every phase has a matching `TI-84_Plus_CE/phase<N>-report.md`. For deep detail on any phase, run `git show <commit>` or read the report.
